This was a big draw because people could make transactions for very small amounts and not incur large fees for it. In December of 2017, there was a huge spike in transaction numbers and that caused a massive increase in fees. Some people were paying up to $40 for one transaction. This is more expensive than the banks and was a major downside to the PoW system that Bitcoin used to approve the transactions. Since then the fees have come down by a lot, however, it has never reached the low level that it started off at. There have also been some increases in fees every now and then. Although, it has never reached the amount it did in 2017 (BitDegree, 2021b).
Right now, the fees are too high to make Bitcoin a viable solution for a global payment system, especially if it grows in popularity. The issues that have been seen with this have been caused by the limitations of the PoW system. There are definitely things that have to be done and worked on in order to deal with the issues put forth. There is always a give and take when it comes to these systems. If Bitcoin decides that it wants to continue on with the Proof of Work system, it would mean that the users will be paying for the security it gives. There might be people who are not happy about that. This is also the reason people are trying out other cryptocurrencies, to see if there is a better option and if they can solve some of the issues that come with PoW. As it stands, there is nothing that has come forward with an overall solution that has the same sort of security that PoW offers.
Bitcoin is not the only cryptocurrency that uses PoW. The second biggest crypto platform, Ethereum, also uses PoW. However, they are looking at changing over to Proof of Stake in the near future. As it stands, the system is still on PoW. Ethereum was able to make some changes to the system to allow transactions to be approved in just 16 seconds, this is significantly quicker than the 10 minutes for Bitcoin. With PoW there are issues with scalability. It makes it difficult to expand the system because that means it will have to increase the number of miners that are doing the work. This might not be sustainable, since there are already environmental impacts that are forming concerns for this. The changes that Ethereum have made to the Bitcoin system has allowed them to process 15 transactions per second. This is more than double the amount that can be done with Bitcoin, but this is still not where it needs to be. If any cryptocurrency wants to be successful as a real-world medium of payment and transactions, it would need to be able to process thousands of transactions per second (BitDegree, 2021b).
Proof of Stake
The alternative to Proof of Work is Proof of Stake (PoS). It aims to solve some of the problems that PoW is facing. At the same time there has been some backlash on it because it may not be as safe and secure as the Proof of Work system. They are two completely different systems that end up with the same result. The amount of resources that needs to be used to mine on a PoS system is much less than on a PoW system. This is due to the way in which it picks who will do the mining.
There is still a cryptographic algorithm being used but the objective of the system is different to the PoW system. At the moment only altcoins are using the PoS systems in their platforms but there has been integration taking place to bring this system into other cryptocurrencies, like Ethereum. The main difference of these two systems is that for PoW the miner is rewarded for solving the equation and needs to use large amounts of computational power and electricity in order to do it. With PoS an individual who mines the block is determined by how many coins they own of the cryptocurrency they are mining. This means that their mining capability is based on the stake of the currency they own (BitDegree, 2021b).
This system means that there aren’t a whole bunch of people trying to mine the exact same block. If someone has 10% of the coins in that specific cryptocurrency, they would only be able to mine 10% of the blocks available. Technically speaking, this can’t be termed as mining because the process is completely different. A person that does not have any of that coin cannot ‘mine’ in a PoS system. This also means that the compensation is different. With PoW the person that is successful and solves the equation will be rewarded with a specific amount of coins that they would have unlocked. With PoS the person who solves the PoS puzzle will be rewarded with the transaction fee for that transaction. These people are often referred to as ‘forgers’ since they do not mine (Frankenfield, 2021c).
Since this system is different from the way mining is done in a PoW system, it would only be fitting to discuss how it is done so that you can get a better idea of what is going on. In order for a forger to have the opportunity to validate the transactions in the systems, they would need to have all of their coins in one, specific wallet. Having the coins all over the palace would make it very difficult to see how many coins the person has and therefore difficult to continue on with this system. The wallet is then frozen and then is used to stake the network. During this time, the forger would not be able to use their coins or take it out of the wallet to spend it. In most cases the forger will need to have a large amount of the coin in order to be able to verify the transactions. There is normally a minimum requirement. This is because it would be difficult for everyone who owns a part of the coin to be involved in this process. The percentages of stake would be too little. This also means that if someone wants to be able to forge, they would need to make a large initial investment into the coin. Only the people who have a good amount of the coin would be allowed to forge and verify transactions. This does limit the amount of people that are allowed to do it and it is not a good way to collect the coins and build up wealth as with PoW.
Assuming that you have enough coins to stake, getting the transaction fee and ‘winning’ is not guaranteed. Your chances of winning the reward is related to the percentage of coins you have staked. So if you have 5% of the coins, you would then have a 5% chance of winning every single reward. With the PoS model the person who gets rewarded is randomly chosen based upon the percentage of coins they own or are able to stake. For those that are able to take part in this process, it is much more cost effective than mining in the PoW system (BitDegree, 2021b).
In order to keep those who put in a stake from doing anything wrong or underhanded, there is a system put in place for this. If the forger is trying to do something they are not supposed to do, like process a transaction that is malicious, they would lose everything they have staked. This is put in place to keep the forgers accountable and to make sure that they are not tempted to do something that could compromise the system. This is the incentive to keep the network secure. The system works so well because the more you stake the more you will earn, but also the coin you will lose if you go against the system policy. People will want to stake as much as they can so that they are able to have a high earning potential, and these are the people that will be the most integrous with what they are doing so that they will not lose what they have staked.
There are many benefits to the PoS system. One of them is that it prevents centralization. This might be confusing since all blockchain systems are decentralized. However, mining operations in the PoW system have the potential to be centralized. We can already see that with Bitcoin. Much of the mining efforts for Bitcoin happens in China because electricity and the mining rigs are cheap there. This is because over half of the mining operations happen in pools. With PoS, collaborative efforts are prevented and this means that a group cannot dominate the network. You will be rewarded depending on the amount that you invest. Only serious investors will benefit from this because they will have to put something in and freeze their coins in order to take part in the process.
The other big benefit to having a PoS system is that it has more protection when it comes to the 51% attack. This is something that can happen with both PoW and PoS systems, but the likelihood is far less with a PoS system. This attack means that a group or individual has obtained 51% or more of the available mining power in the system. They now have the power to make changes and alter the block for their own gain. With the PoS system doing this would not make much financial sense. You would first have to have 51% of the available coin in the system. Then you would have to put it into the wallet to freeze while you perform your malicious actions. The network would quickly realize what had happened and the bad guy would lose all of their coin. Not only that, but if someone were to try and buy that much of the coin, the price would keep increasing in real-world value. At the end of the day, what they have spent on getting the 51% of the coin would far supersede the amount that they would be getting back from trying to hack the system. There would then really be no point in doing this since the risk versus the reward just does not make sense. The only reason someone would do this is just to be malicious and not anything else. The person would also have to be extremely wealthy so that they have that much money to put on the line and not care about losing it. This scenario is extremely unlikely, so the PoS system is much safer than the PoW system in this regard (BitDegree, 2021b).
With the advantages, there are some disadvantages that have been put forward with regards to the PoS system. One of them is that it runs on a ‘the rich get richer’ system. You have to have a certain number of coins in order to invest and stake your coin. Once you invest you have the chance to make more coins because you will get paid out based on what you have. The more you have, the more you will get. Most people will not be able to invest in this as they will not have enough to do so. The other downside is that transactions can be verified on different chains. This allows a higher chance for a hacker to perform a double spend attack. While this is not a huge concern, the likelihood of this happening is more than with a PoW system.
The argument between the two is something that is ongoing and seems to divide people quite a bit. There is a good chance that we won’t see a resolution between the two for quite a while. There are also developments that could happen to improve one or the other. At the end of the day, the one that a cryptocurrency goes for will depend on the developers and what they deem the most efficient and safe for what they are trying to achieve. There is no doubt that there will be many different developments coming out as cryptocurrency continues to grow. These two are not the only systems that have been tried out or been put in place in the world of cryptocurrency. Who knows, there might be a completely new system that comes out and takes the market by storm. This is not an outrageous scenario when you think about how quickly things are developing and how fast new innovations are being added to the mix.

Chapter 9:
Ethereum—The Quick Rise to Popularity
Ethereum is the second most popular cryptocurrency out there. It was inspired by Bitcoin, but aims to better what the creators feel are the shortfalls that come with Bitcoin. From its inception, it has had a quick rise to popularity. This is partly because Bitcoin has opened the door to cryptocurrency and people are more receptive to it.
Who Created Ethereum?
The creator of Ethereum is Vitalik Buterin. He is a Canadian-Russian coder who was very interested in cryptocurrency from quite a young age. In 2011, he co-founded a website called Bitcoin Magazine. Here he published many articles on Bitcoin and cryptocurrency. He was definitely passionate about the subject. He was only 19 years old at the time. At that point in most of our lives, we were probably interested in more trivial things and he was on the road to creating one of the biggest cryptocurrency platforms in the world. That is quite an impressive feat (Hertig, 2021).
He was in university at this time, but two years later he decided to drop out and pursue the Bitcoin Magazine full time. He focused on learning more about blockchain and the capabilities of it. The more he learnt, the more he realized that blockchain was capable of much more than just peer-to-peer currency. His idea was to create a blockchain platform with a built-in programming language. This would allow people to be able to use the platform to build other kinds of applications with the technology (Frank & Silverstein, 2019).
In 2013, he released a white paper that gave the details of his idea. He wanted to design this platform for a decentralized application someone wanted to build. This was a very attractive idea and still is because a decentralized platform would be accessible to people from all parts of the world. This would allow a greater reach and has benefits to both the user and the owner of the application. Just like the Bitcoin blockchain platform, this would also give the user more control over their personal information and data.
While Vitalik Buterin was the mastermind behind Ethereum, he isn’t the only one who had a hand in the developing and building of the platform. He had help from quite a few investors and developers along the way. The two other co-founders of Ethereum are Dr. Gavin Wood and Joseph Lubin. Dr. Wood did much of the programming and designing of the Ethereum platform in the early days of development. Later on in his career he founded Polkadot, which we will talk about later on in the book. Joseph Lubin was also heavily involved in the development of the platform and has also moved on to create many other tech-based companies, one of which is ConsenSys.
Along the way, there were many other people that played a part in the building, development, and designing of the Ethereum platform. However, there three men put in the most work and ended up being the co-founders of the finished product of the platform. Most big technology platforms and companies are not built with just one person. This is why there are many people who think that the inventor of Bitcoin, Satoshi Nakamoto, is actually an alias for a group of people rather than one person. This is believable, since we can see the Ethereum platform and many others like it have had many different people put their skills in the mix. The Ethereum founders are a lot more open about themselves and their lives than Satoshi Nakamoto.
The Reason Behind the Creation of Ethereum
Vitalik Buterin had an invested interest in Bitcoin and blockchain technology since he first heard of it. After extensive research, he realized that the technology could be used for so much more than what Bitcoin was utilizing it for. He never thought that Bitcoin was badly designed or that it was not working the way it should be, instead he saw the future possibility of the software itself. That is why he created Ethereum. He wanted to do something with the blockchain software that could really use it in a way that is not as limited as Bitcoin is.
Buterin once stated in an interview that it is not that Bitcoin is very limited in its functionality. He made the comparison between a pocket calculator and a smartphone. You can use the calculator to work out all the math problems you need to, but that is pretty much all it is capable of. Whereas the smartphone can have a calculator app on it and do the exact same thing as the calculator, but is also capable of doing much more. You can take pictures, listen to music, browse the web, use it to communicate with others, and much more. It is not that the calculator is bad at its job, it just only has one job. His argument is, why just have a calculator (Bitcoin) when you can have a smartphone (Ethereum). It’s taking the same idea of Bitcoin, but increasing the power and functionality so that it has a more general and flexible purpose (Frank & Silverstein, 2019).
The goal here is to have a platform that supports the development of many apps, sites, and platforms. Decentralization isn’t just a good thing for finances, but also for many other sectors and areas. If you look at the recent uproar that has been going on with regards to Facebook and the selling of data to other companies, you would see that the world is ready for more privacy. People would be more in control of their data and how it is being used. The only information that would be able to be shared is the information that the user shares on their own accord. This will make apps similar to Facebook and Instagram have to change the way they do things and give users more control over their information.
Developers could build applications that have no need for a leader or central control. This is handing control back to the end users. This means that the user can feel safe and secure knowing that their data cannot be tampered with or sold. Another benefit of these kinds of applications is that there is virtually no risk of being hacked and the users information being stolen. There is always a huge risk of this when working with a centralized system. A hacker only has to hack one place in order to have access to all of the user’s information. Having a decentralized system makes this harder and therefore protects the users better.
Bitcoin vs Ethereum
Since Bitcoin and Ethereum are at the top of the log when it comes to decentralized finances and blockchain, many people need to know the differences between them. This is not going to show why one is better than the other. Instead, it is going to highlight the differences they have. If they were both exactly the same, one would end up being irrelevant.
They both have some similarities that allow them to work the way they do. Ethereum was inspired by Bitcoin, so it is only natural that there would be similarities between them. Some of these similarities are that they both run on a ledger system, and they both work using blockchain. Both of these have been touched on quite in-depth earlier on in the book so will we not be going through them here. You can trade with both of these via online exchanges and store them in cryptocurrency wallets. There is a lot of overlap in information with these two platforms. Knowing the differences is the best way to understand Ethereum and why it has taken off so quickly.
The thing that drives the differences in the platforms is the end goal of each of them. They are not chasing after the same end goal and that is why they differ in certain ways. Bitcoin was designed as a way to store up wealth and transact in an easier way. Another main driver for the Bitcoin platform was that it is able to keep the users details anonymous, adding to the security of the platform. Bitcoin was made to replace real-world money to some extent. This is not the goal of Ethereum. While some of these goals can be met in the way Ethereum has been set up, it is not the main driver of the platform. Ethereum was designed to become a platform where other decentralized apps and smart contracts could run from, this will all be done via its own currency. While Ethereum is a digital currency, the goal is not to be used as a monetary system that takes over or provides an alternative to the one we are using right now. The aim is to facilitate and monetize the smart contract and decentralized application platform (Reiff, 2020).
Something else that is very different between the two is that they have a different supply structure. You would know by now that Bitcoin will only ever have 21 million coins in circulation. This is so that Bitcoin can lean on the same standard as the gold standard and prevent things like hyperinflation and the government and financial institutions from having control over the value of money. With Ethereum there is no cap on the amount that is available in the world. It is mined similar to Bitcoin, but this process can be continued indefinitely since there will never be a stop to the amount of Ethereum coins in the world (Luno, n.d.).
There are quite a few other smaller differences when it comes to Bitcoin and Ethereum. They both use different algorithms to process their transactions and other aspects of their programs. You will also find that Ethereum transactions are processed much quicker than Bitcoin. It’s not that Bitcoin will take ages, but Ethereum approves transactions in a few seconds compared to the minutes needed for Bitcoin.
When Ethereum was created it was not to be in competition with Bitcoin, but to be a system for a blockchain that supports it. There really should not be any competition between the two because they are essentially doing different things. However, the ether has risen in popularity and that is what has pushed it to be in competition with the other cryptocurrencies. Traders have become very interested in it and that is why, from an investment point of view it has been seen as a very good competitor on the market. It is important to note that the ecosystem in which the ether runs is about 10 times smaller than that of Bitcoin, this influences the market cap of the ether (Reiff, 2020).
While these two might seem very similar for those who just look in, they are not. They are built on the same foundation and idea, but after that, you can see where their paths diverge. As the platforms evolve and develop, we should expect to see even more changes as they step into their full potentials. Right now it is good to know the difference so that you can be aware of any changes that happen as development continues.
Using Ethereum and Ether
Ethereum is often called the world’s computer because of the functionality available. The common consensus in the world of tech and cryptocurrency is that if the developments continue on as expected, it is very likely that Ethereum will become an alternative platform for things like Facebook and Google. It would offer the same functionality, but would be a lot more secure to the end user. Most people love the current platforms, but have a problem with the privacy factors; this kind of innovation could solve that problem.
These alternatives will not be free. The user will have to pay for access and use these platforms with ether. Almost all of the platforms we use today are free to some extent. We pay for it by giving our information to the company or by viewing the ads that companies and organizations pay for. These ads are the ones that pop up all over our screen. Organizations will pay for that ad space and that is how the online platform rakes in the cash. With an Ethereum app every action will come at a cost. If you want to post something on one of the apps or send a message, it will cost you a small amount of ether. The fees will allow you to gain access to the apps and use the platform (Hertig, 2017).
The reason users have to pay for access to these apps is because of the computing power that is needed to power Ethereum. The more users that use the platform the higher the fees will be, because there is more power needed to sustain the activity. This is not a cheap feat and because there are no ads or anything like that, there is no way to fund this operation but to have users pay in. As it stands, the fees are quite high because of the amount of apps and users that go through the platform. There is definitely a shortfall in the Ethereum platform in terms of scalability. There is an active plan in the works with the launch of Ethereum 2.0. This will hopefully cause a drop in fees and keep the security of the platform quite high.
Anyone who has ether and has a device that is connected to the internet can access Ethereum and all the apps on it. These apps will not look exactly like the ones that are being used regularly today. However, they are improving. There have been massive improvements in the way these apps function and the platform in general. One must remember that all the kinks have not been worked out as yet because it is a pretty new platform and idea as a whole. It shows a lot of promise, but any person that gets involved with Ethereum must understand that there will be changes, development, and problem solving that will still need to happen in order to get it to the space that we want it to be.
This all leads to the all-important question of, how does one use ether? The process of obtaining and spending ether is quite similar to that of Bitcoin. There are very similar processes in place that will allow for the secure transitioning and saving of ether. In order to get ether you will need an Ethereum wallet. This is the exact same as a Bitcoin wallet, where you are able to store your ethers to spend at a later stage. These wallets can come in the form of a desktop, mobile, hardware, and paper wallets. This is all exactly the same as the Bitcoin wallets and can be used in the same way as well, so we will not go in depth with it since the process has already been covered earlier on in the book.
Once you have a wallet to store your ether, you need to buy some. This can be done by various different methods that are also very similar to Bitcoin. You can actually purchase ether on various different platforms that other cryptocurrencies are available from. The easiest way to buy them is using a direct centralized exchange. Coinbase and Kraken are popular and reliable exchanges where you can buy your crypto from. You can buy ether by using your dollars or even Bitcoin. If you are not from America or you want to use another currency to buy your ether, there will most likely be another step involved that will ask you to convert your currency. This instruction will be given to you via the exchange company. If your country or currency is able to buy Bitcoin you can do that and then exchange it for ether, this seems to be the easiest way to go about it (Hertig, 2017).
The other ways you could purchase ether is through a cryptocurrency ATM or via a direct transaction with someone who is selling ether. The draw for ATMs is that you can use cash to buy your ether and the transaction is immediate. You will be able to buy many different cryptocurrencies from an ATM like this but you will need to find one that is near to you, and this can be a challenge. The other option, which is a direct transaction with a seller, can be the most difficult for most people. You will have to find a seller and then be able to meet up with them in order to make the exchange. Big cities like Toronto and New York have frequent meet ups that allow people to trade in person, but for those that live in smaller cities or in rural areas, this could definitely be a problem. You can also check the Ethereum website for their list of official ways to buy ether. It will be updated should other options become available.
Once you have your ether, you can spend it on the platform. You will have your pick of decentralized apps to choose from and you can use it how you please. There are quite a few options ranging from games to social media type apps. One of the games is called Cryptokitties, where you can breed and collect digital kittens. It is like those games where you have to keep your pet alive. Another app that is available is one called Peepeth and it is similar to Twitter. Anything posted to the platform cannot be deleted and your account will cannot be deleted for any reason like Twitter is able to do. There are monitors who check what is posted to make sure that it is not spam and that it is appropriate content. There are also plenty of decentralized finance related apps that have been built in the system that can be used whenever the user wants to do so.
As the platform grows and develops there will be more available for users to do and be a part of. At this point there is nothing that is big enough to take over any of the popular apps that are now in the real-world. It will take some time before this becomes something that a majority of people get on board with, if that ever does happen. There have been lots of ideas and developments put forward to make the platform better. It is no secret that the development process of Ethereum is quite quick and there is no sign of it slowing down.
Ethereum Smart Contracts
If you are trying to understand how Ethereum works, you will need to understand smart contracts. This is because a smart contract is the basis of the platform and the whole point to why it was built in the first place. A smart contract serves the same purpose as a regular paper contract, but there is no need for an intermediary.
In the real world, if you wanted to buy something or do a deal with the bank, there is a good chance that you would have to sign a contract for it. This standard contract would state the terms in which the money may be transferred, the fees involved, what you will be getting in return, and a whole lot of other terms and conditions. This keeps each party accountable and each will know what is expected of them. If there is a breach in contract, the contract can be terminated and the transaction can no longer take place. A smart contract fulfills a similar purpose, but without all the added involvement of the users. The smart contract makes it possible to encode the terms and conditions in which the money can be moved and transacted within the money itself. This is why there is no need for an intermediary.
Bitcoin does use smart contracts for the same purpose but Ethereum is looking to expand on this and include other types of transactions, such as a loan option. This will allow there to be more options on the platform. It will still be decentralized and it would allow more flexibility on the platform as well as more real-world uses. There are already tests being done with certain programs on the Ethereum app that will allow this to be a possibility. The flexibility that is being integrated into smart contracts is one of the primary innovations that Ethereum has over Bitcoin. Ethereum is far from overtaking Bitcoin in the crypto space as there is still a lot of fine tuning that needs to be done. Other cryptocurrencies are also working with smart contracts to make them more flexible. It is basically a race to be the first of its kind to take up space in this area.
Ethereum Mining
At the moment, Ethereum mining is very similar to mining Bitcoin since they both use the PoW system. The reason mining is so important is that it allows the ether to be minted without the need for a central authority and it keeps the network running how it should. This process is integral to the way Ethereum is built and works. If there were no mining taking place, it would not be possible for the platform to function. Before Satoshi Nakamoto, the inventor of Bitcoin, came up with the idea of mining there were many other cryptocurrencies that were launched and they ended up failing. This is why most other cryptocurrencies, after the launch of Bitcoin, followed in its footsteps.
When mining ether, a block is found every 12–15 seconds. This is the range that it has to stay in for it to be sustainable. If it happens slower or quicker than this, then the algorithm adjusts itself so that it brings it back into this range. Just like with Bitcoin, you have a higher chance of being successful with it when you purchase higher end equipment that have higher hash rates. You also have the option of joining a mining pool to increase the chances of you solving the mathematical equation that needs to be solved so that you are rewarded with ether.
The mining process of ether is almost exactly the same as that of Bitcoin. If you are able to understand that, then there is virtually no additional information that needs to be taken in with regards to the mining of ether. However, Ethereum has announced that they will be moving over to a Proof of Stake system rather than sticking to the Proof of Work system. They have stated that there will be a number of added benefits and that it will help with the scalability of the platform. Ethereum has struggled with scaling the platform to where they would like it to be, due to the limitations that are present on the currency system. This will completely change the way ether is mined. We have already discussed the mining process of a cryptocurrency when it is on a PoS system versus a PoW system. We do not know if there will be any variation in the way Ethereum will do things because they have not released much information on how it will be run as of yet. Once they fully launch Ethereum 2.0, we will get the full scope of what the mining process will look like as well as any other changes that will be taking place with the platform.
Ethereum 2.0
There has been a lot of buzz around the launch of Ethereum 2.0. There is going to be some definite benefits to it and people are excited to see what the new platform has in store for them. Technically Ethereum 2.0 has already launched a few months ago, but this is just the first phase. The plan is to roll it out in three separate phases, the last one being completed somewhere close to the end of 2021 or into 2022. There has not been a definite date being set, but there have already been changes in the way things are being done.
The gradual phasing in of the stages means that Ethereum is still running on PoW and that PoS will be slowly phased in. There is no exact timeline to when this will happen but in the next year or two seems to be the general consensus. This is still subject to change and may take longer. It all depends on the development process and if they hit any snags along the way.
The big questions are what is Ethereum 2.0 and how will it differ from the current system? We will not know the full scope of this until it is fully implemented, but the information we have will lead us to a pretty close guess to how it will look in the future. Ethereum 2.0, or Serenity, as it has also been called, is an upgrade on the already existing Ethereum system. The reason this has been implemented is to increase the speed and efficiency of the system so that it can be used on a broader scale. The scalability has been stunted by the current system so the developers believe the changes they are making will solve this problem and make the platform a lot more functionable for a long time into the future.
The two major changes that will take place is the changeover to Proof of Stake, which we have already spoken about, and the addition of something called sharding. Sharding refers to a process that will allow the splitting up of one blockchain into many different blockchains. This will make the system a lot more efficient since there can be different validators for each shard, rather than one validator that has to handle the whole work load. The validator will then only have to have information that relates to its shard. The Beacon Chain is implemented so that there can be communication between the chains (EC-Council, 2021).
The Beacon Chain was the first step in the launch of Ethereum 2.0. It is currently out and running. It will eventually introduce the PoS system at a later time. Changing from one system over to another is not an easy feat. The developers will still need to keep the old system usable until the new one is completely ready. It would not be possible to shut down the whole Ethereum platform and bring out the new one. The users will lose out and it would have a negative effect on the price of ethers, which would not be favorable for the company.
With this being said, you probably want to know what the Beacon Chain actually does and what the benefits of this are. The Beacon Chain acts almost like a lighthouse in a sea of data. It is the heart of the program because it is the part of the system that scans through the data, validates, collects votes, and gives out the rewards to those that mine ether. How the user interacts with the platform has not changed. Anything that you would have done before the introduction of the Beacon Chain can still be done now. It will stay this way until Ethereum 2.0 is completely ready to be launched, so there is no need to start learning anything new with regards to the system just yet. The Beacon Chain is in charge of the coordination of the new blocks that will be added to the system. It needs to make sure that they are all valid and keeps the network safe and secure (Beck, 2020).
Since the Beacon Chain system has been implemented, the next step in the process will be to introduce the sharding chains. There will be 64 separate shards to which the data will be spread across. This is not necessarily a new system as Ethereum already breaks up their data into smaller chunks, this is just an add on to the system that will allow for a further split in data and allow for more scalability and more efficient handling of the workload overall. The shards will not be used for the execution of codes, their functionality is just limited to the storing and accessing of data in the system. Once the shards have been launched, the merge between the current Ethereum system and Ethereum 2.0 will not be far behind (Beck, 2020).
While everything seems to be going according to plan and it is on track for a full launch at the end of 2021 or the beginning of 2022, there might be a change in the plan laid forth. This is standard when it comes to big projects like this. There is still a lot of research that has to be done before a major move like this happens. The developers will have to decide what is the best move forward as new research pops up. The worst thing would be to launch it prematurely and have something go wrong and ruin the credibility of the platform. The best thing to do is to keep abreast with what is going on with the official Ethereum communications. There will be a lot of speculation until the system has launched or until Ethereum gives an indication of what is going on. While it may be fun and informative to read opinion pieces, it is never good to take these as law. Be cautious when it comes to these.
Only once the official communication has been sent out, will we know the exact steps forward and where the developers stand. There should be some communication well in advance of the launch and merge of the systems so that users will be able to adequately prepare for it. There will also be instructions on any change in the way things are done so that the users won’t be left in the dark. When it comes to things like this communication is usually on point to avoid any confusion that may take place.
This is definitely an exciting and bold move by Ethereum and one that looks like it is going to give a good pay off for the company and for the users. This could be the move that pushed Ethereum closer to Bitcoin in terms of the world's biggest cryptocurrency. While Ethereum does not have the same goals as Bitcoin, this might change in time. You can never be sure with this. One thing we can be almost certain about is that if the change in the Ethereum platform turns out to be massively successful, many other cryptocurrencies and blockchain platforms will want to follow suit. This could be the start of a large movement into the PoS way of doing things.

Chapter 10:
Polkadot—The New Kid on the Block
Since its launch in 2020, Polkadot has risen in popularity and there have been a lot of investments made into it. This means that there are many in the tech community that find that this model very attractive and can see the benefits of it in the long term. It does take a lot of its inspiration from Ethereum, but it is not an exact replica. There have been many developments that have made some differentiations between the two. The goals of the systems are not the same, which is why Polkadot has attracted many investors and has piqued the interest of many developers.
Polkadot is not in any way trying to overtake Ethereum. Instead, it’s trying to create a platform that will be able to work with Ethereum and other blockchain platforms. It aims to be the bridge between all different blockchain technologies. This is definitely an interesting goal as we have not seen anything like this in the blockchain world. It could definitely be a game changer and its investment made into it shows that people are really buying into the idea.
Polkadot is definitely not trying to become the next big cryptocurrency in the market. There is no risk that it will overtake Bitcoin as they are not competing in the same arena. Bitcoins main focus is to become an alternative to the banking and financial services industry. The developers at Polkadot have never indicated that they are interested in something similar. Their main focus is the platform and then using the cryptocurrency side of it as a medium to trade with the platform. This is very similar to the way Ethereum does things. This seems to be the new way to go in cryptocurrency as there is not many others out there in the market that want to have the same end goal as Bitcoin.
There is still some development that needs to be done on the platform, but this is standard across all the crypto platforms. Polkadot is more on par with Ethereum 2.0 rather than the Ethereum platform that is currently being used. This is because Polkadot uses Proof of Stake instead of Proof of Work. This serves the purposes of Polkadot much better and brings with it benefits to the way the system is run. If you have never heard of Polkadot up until today, that is understandable. It is not as mainstream as Bitcoin or Ethereum. However, there is no doubt that it will be a big name in the crypto and blockchain arena.
The Developers Behind the Platform
There are three people that are behind the development of the Polkadot platform. The platform is a flagship project by a Swiss foundation called the Web 3 Foundation. This is a group of three guys one of which is Dr. Gavin Wood, the co-founder of Ethereum. He has had his hand in many areas of blockchain so it seems fitting that he has a big part to play here. He is the head of innovation at Polkadot and the president of the Web 3 Foundation.
The second of the founders is Robert Habermeier. His function is based on the language of coding that is being used throughout the system. The third and final founder of the Polkadot platform is Peter Czaban. He is the director of technology at the Web 3 Foundation and works very closely with the development of the next generation of technologies (Polkadot Network, n.d.).
Dr. Wood left Ethereum in order to start his own venture, which led him to starting Polkadot along with a few other companies in the tech space. At first glance, it might seem that Polkadot is the exact same thing as Ethereum and that it is looking to that place that Ethereum holds in the market. It is definitely true that these two share a lot of similarities, however, the goal of Polkadot is not to replace Ethereum, but to work with it. Polkadot is working to build interoperability between different chains.
How Does It Work?
Polkadot has actually grown incredibly fast compared to other cryptocurrencies in the market. It is not just designed to be a cryptocurrency, but to work as a platform for other blockchain apps to be built upon. In this way it is very similar to Ethereum. Polkadot was only launched in 2020 and it already shows a lot of promise. Instead of just running on one kind of blockchain, it is run by two different types of blockchains. This is a unique and innovative design that has really made it stand out amongst its competitors.
The two blockchains that this is run on are called the relay chains and the parachain. The relay chain is the main chain or main network. This is where the transactions are verified and made permanent. The second type of chains are called the parachains. These are where the users can create networks of their own. They can use this part of the network to build upon and create apps and so on. These smaller chains benefit from the same type of security as the main chain because it feeds into the main chain. They can also be customized for a variety of different uses, which makes it a very flexible way to use the blockchain technology (Kraken, n.d.).
The relay chain of Polkadot is the foundational or main chain of the system. It runs on the Proof of Stake system, but it uses a modified version of it so that it better suits the needs of the network. This consensus is called Nominated Proof of Stake. This is a very interesting concept but it still relies on the basics of a PoS network. There are four very important roles that need to be fulfilled in order for the platform to operate correctly. These roles are the validator, nominators, collectors, and fishermen. When someone stakes their coin into the system, they are assigned and locked into one or more of these roles.
Each of these roles perform a different function, but they are all rewarded in the same way, with DOT, which is the name of the token used on the Polkadot platform. Validators are responsible for validating data in the parachain blocks. They can also vote on any changes that might need to happen to better the system. Nominators act to make sure that the relay chain is secure by selecting validators that are trustworthy. A nominator would have to take the DOT tokens that they have staked and delegate it to a validator and this will count as a vote for that validator. Collectors act as the connector from the parachains to the relay chain. It collects all the data and stores the full history of the parachains to be aggregated into blocks that can then be added to the relay chain. Lastly, the fishermen are those that monitor and watch the network for any suspicious behavior. If any of this is found it reports this back to the validators.
The parachains each have their own specific function and purpose within the overall system of the blockchain. They are also independent blockchains that just run on top of the Relay chain or the main chain. You can think of this like having one chain dedicated to the smart contracts of the system, one for providing a coin that is stable enough to be used for payments, and another one that will focus on beginning an energy system into the network. There will only be a specific amount of paper chains that are available in the network. If someone wants to own a slot or a chain they will have to compete in a type of public auction where the potential owners will go up against each other so that they can be chosen to obtain one of their own slots. This will prevent just having too many parachains that the network would not be able to handle or keep tabs on them. In the future, the amount of parachains in the network will increase to accommodate more systems being built in, but this will depend on how many are needed and how many the main chain can handle (Polkadotters | Kusama validators, 2020).
A smaller version of a parachain also exists in the system. This is called a parathread. This is the chain that will allow a space in the network to be leased out for a specific amount of time so that regular people will be able to build and run projects on Polkadot. There will be no actions taking place for this, someone will just be able to rent out the parathread slot for the time that they need it for their project. This is also a great way for someone to test out the network before they commit to purchasing an expensive parachain in the system. They will be able to build what they envision and see if it will work the way they want it to without having to commit to a full parachain. The user could also run their project on a parathread while they are waiting for a parachain slot to open up for them or until they have the resources to enter the auction for one. This provides an option for those that want to get involved, but do not have all the resources necessary to commit to it (Polkadotters | Kusama validators, 2020).
While those are the chains that are currently available, there is also a third chain that can be used with this network. This is called a bridge. Bridges will allow the Polkadot network to interact with other blockchain networks. As it stands, each blockchain network is only able to trade with itself and connect with itself. If two blockchain networks are connected by a bridge the user would be able to interact between the two. This will open up the door to a whole host of other possibilities. You would be able to trade your crypto coins with another network without having to go through a central exchange. This will also be a great thing for merchants. Let’s say a merchant only accepts Bitcoin and you only have the DOT token. You could still trade with the merchant through the bridge. Your DOT would be converted into Bitcoin and the merchant would then receive the Bitcoin. This will allow for a lot more flexibility in the system and people would get to choose which cryptocurrency they want to invest in without being limited to where they can trade.
The Goal of Polkadot
The ultimate goal of Polkadot is to become the internet of blockchain. If you look at how the internet works, it is able to connect many different devices so that the users can communicate and send digital files to each other. Someone can send an email on their computer in London, England and it will be picked up by the receiver’s smartphone who is in Johannesburg, South Africa. Even though there is a huge distance between them, the message still comes through and it is done in a few seconds. Not only that but the internet allows the users to communicate using different devices. You can send a message from a computer to a tablet, to a phone, and even to a server of something like a home security system. This is what the Polkadot platform wants to do with blockchain. It wants to be the medium to which all other blockchains are connected and can communicate with each other.
The reasoning behind this that the creators of Polkadot believe that while there are many great blockchain technologies out there, none of them can fully fulfill all purposes. As much as each platform might try to become as well rounded as possible and serve all purposes, they will ultimately fail at it. Most blockchains are really good at doing one thing and the cost of that is that the other services do not get that much attention or are not able to be fulfilled due to the restrictions imposed on it by the main function. If you look at Ethereum and Bitcoin, they are both incredibly sophisticated software that are really good in their own spaces. However, since Bitcoin’s main focus is to provide an alternative system to the current financial system it does not have the freedom to move into other areas and become a platform that other people can build upon. If it were to do this, there might be some security issues that arise and it would defeat the purpose of Bitcoin. On the other hand Ethereum does have the freedom to expand and allow other people to build their own blockchain technology on their system because they are not focused on providing a real-world solution to banking and finances. They both operate on different levels, but if they are brought together it will make transacting and iterating much easier. There will be a lot more flexibility and security if each blockchain company was able to just focus on their own thing, without being everything at once.
A real-world example of this would be a clothing retailer and a bank. They both provide services to the public and they both deal with money and finances, even though it is on different levels. A bank would find it very difficult to operate as a retailer as this means that it would have to compromise on certain aspects. Security would be a big problem since there would be so much going on and people would be everywhere. It would be easier for a criminal to sneak in and steal and perform some other malicious act. The bank workers would then have to split up their time between tending to the floor and actually performing their banking activities. It would not be long before this hybrid bank and retail store would have to close down because they would not be able to serve all functions that they claim to be able to do. When they are separated they are able to perform their designated functions in a much more thorough way. A person could still use the bank as a way to keep their money and then pay for their items at the retail store using their bank card. There is still an overlap, but each company is able to focus on their strongest function and still be connected to the other industries. Polkadot wants to be that connection in the blockchain world.
This goal is why so many people are on board with Polkadot. It offers a solution to a problem that many blockchain companies have been facing. It is also going to make it easier for those companies to thrive and provide the services that they want to provide. This will also improve the scalability of blockchain as a whole. There have been many problems in terms of scalability when it comes to blockchain. Many companies have become stagnant or fees suddenly skyrocket because it is unable to scale as quickly as the number of users increase in the system. Polkadot will bring all the different blockchains under one roof and will therefore be able to provide the best platform for whatever the users project needs are (Polkadotters | Kusama validators, 2020).
The Problems Polkadot Are Solving
Now that you know what Polkadot is and where it is headed, let’s talk about the benefits of using this platform. Now this does not mean that other platforms are somehow inferior, but there are some definite benefits to this platform that others just don’t have. With the way technology works, others might want to develop something similar into their platforms or new blockchain platforms may copy or build upon the Polkadot system.
The reason Polkadot was developed was to act as a solution to many problems that other blockchain platforms face. The challenges that these platforms face usually stop them from growing past certain points and put roadblocks in their way that take time to solve. The benefit of not being the first to create a system is that you can see what the potential problems are in the other systems and then find ways to build something better and something that will be able to mitigate these issues. This is why Polkadot was able to build the platform that it did and why it has found so many solutions to the common problems being put forward in the blockchain community.
There are five main problems that keep popping up when it comes to blockchain platforms. These problems are scaling, customization, interoperability, governance, and upgradeability. Whenever there is something that prevents a blockchain system from moving forward, there is a good chance that it is caused by one of these things. Of course, there are always plans in motion to fix the problems, but that can take some time because the solutions have to be integrated into an already working program. Having something that already solves the potential issued from the jump is a very attractive alternative and is one of the major reasons that Polkadot has risen in popularity so quickly. Let’s talk about each of these problems and how Polkadot has been able to solve them one at a time (Binance Academy, 2021b).
The first one is scalability. This has been the problem with both of the blockchain platforms we have spoken about in this book. Bitcoin will most likely struggle to scale as the mining equipment is causing problems with electricity usage and the environment. Ethereum has also reached a ceiling when it comes to scalability, however, they have planned to solve this with Ethereum 2.0. Polkadot, on the other hand, is a very scalable platform. The reason for this is because it runs on a multichain network. This allows the processes and transactions that take place in the system to run parallel to each other at the same time. This means that more than one block can be approved in a specific space of time because there are more than one chain working on this process. This is a major benefit to the system because it means that the scalability is unlimited. This could easily be the start of a global blockchain adoption process.
The next problem is customization. This has more to do with systems that want to offer avenues to build your own programs and projects using the blockchain system. With most platforms customization is limited because there is no one blockchain system that can fit every single need. Every project will need something different and has their own set of requirements that need to be met in order for the project to progress and be successful. If there is not an option to have something specific to certain projects, the amount of development and new projects that can be held by the system will stagnate. Polkadot allows each individual chain to have its design changed and optimized to fit the functionality that is needed for the project. This means that a developer can come in and customize the chain in order to fit the requirements that need to be met in order for them to build their project in the optimal way. This allows customization over a broad spectrum and it will increase the innovation and the amount of projects that can be developed using blockchain technology.
Another big problem is interoperability. This just means the ability of a network to share data with another network. Right now, each blockchain technology runs on its own system and there is no way for each of them to communicate with another. If someone has ether and wants to trade it for Bitcoin, they would have to go through a central exchange to do it. Here, they would hand in their ether and then receive the Bitcoin according to the exchange rate. This may not be a physical ‘bank’ type operation, as you can do it online, but it does add another step to the process. This is not the only problem that can be solved with interoperability between different systems. It is yet to be seen what kind of systems could be built or what the possibilities are. However, Polkadot has managed to add this extra layer onto blockchain development and this could easily expand the uses for blockchain in the future.
The next problem is one that has to do with governance. Since there is only one main blockchain network in most blockchain platforms, it means that everything has to run through that. Creating separate chains allows each chain to belong to a community. This community can govern their chain as they see fit. They won’t be limited to the rules that would be enforced by another community. This allows for flexibility in the way things are done. Polkadot will also be able to gather information and insight from the different communities and this will help with the governance of the platform as a whole. This will allow any future developments to be more well suited to the communities that use the platform. Projects will be better equipped to evolve and improve over time. As the platform grows and there are more users who join, this will be crucial for the future governance of the platform as a whole. It is almost like the systems put in place by governments of countries. There are smaller states within the country and each state has different needs that have to be taken into account when making a rule or law on a national level. There is also the option for the states to make adjustments and laws according to their needs that do not affect the other states that will not benefit from a change. In the end, it will result in a much smoother running of the platform and much happier users as well.
The final problem that Polkadot has solved is the issue of upgrading. There is a lot of work that goes into upgrading a system. Even if you just want to deal with a certain portion of the system, you will have to take into account the rest of the system as it could be affected by the upgrade. The way Polkadot is set up allows upgrades to happen in a specific chain without causing any hard forks. The difference between hard fork and soft forks is a bit of a long-winded topic, so we will discuss that in the next section. For now you should know that the system allows for the chain that needs to be upgraded to do so without causing friction in any other chain or area of the system.
Hard Forks vs Soft Forks
These forks all refer to the when there is an update made to the system. When an update is made, there has to be a change to something in the program. This is usually done to make the platform function better and be able to handle whatever innovations that are being added. Updates are essential in the tech world because things are always changing. It doesn't take too long for a piece of technology, a program, or a system to become obsolete because of new information and innovation that has hit the market. This is easily seen in everyday life. Every so often a new iPhone or gaming console comes out that functions better and has even better features than the last one. Everyone will run to the new version because it is just better. It is also why phones have to update their software every so often. There has been a bug that has been fixed or something has been added that will make the user experience even better. Without the updates all of these pieces of technology would become obsolete and be taken over with something better. In order to stay relevant in this competitive market, updates are necessary.
There is a difference with the way updates are handled on a smartphone and on a cryptocurrency platform. When your phone is ready for an update all you usually have to do is click the terms and condition and accept the update. It will update automatically from there. Now you have the option to deny the update. If you do this, then your phone will just not update, but the repercussions of this is that your phone’s software might not be compatible with other downloads and you might get kicked out from certain apps. This is not ideal, so that is why we update. The updates are decided by the company and if you want to use their product you will need to update. There really isn’t much choice for the user.
With cryptocurrency and blockchain, things work a little differently. You don’t have to read all the terms and conditions or even understand anything that underpins the software and the platform can’t really force any changes on the user. All updates have to be agreed upon by the majority of the users on the platform. This does add a level of difficulty to the updates as there is a longer process involved in order to get it done and sometimes the majority will not rule in favor of the update and then it would not be able to be implemented. In order to initiate an update, there are two different mechanisms that can be used. These are hard forks and soft forks.
Before we get into what these are, let’s figure out what a fork is as a whole. The full name for this is a software fork. This is created anytime the software is copied or modified. The original software still exists but there is now a new branch in it. Essentially the software forks off into another direction. All the projects will have a common starting point and share the same history, but each would splinter off into its own section that would be able to carry and support the new innovation and ideas. This split creates a permanent divergence in the paths of the software. For example, if there is a program and half of the developers want to keep it the same and the other want to add a specific update, they can separate into two groups. Their paths are now separate and eventually will start looking very different from how it all started. New ideas and innovation can be added as development continues. There can be an unlimited amount of forks in this process. This process is not limited to blockchain. It has been happening with almost all open-source projects. However, the concept of hard and soft forks is something that can almost only be found in a blockchain network (Binance Academy, 2021a).
With a hard fork, there is a radical change that happens. Typically what would happen is that the nodes would add new rules to the system that directly conflicts with the old rules. This means that they would not be able to work together simultaneously. With these big changes some want to stay with the old rules and others would move over to the new rules. The new rules would then have to create a separate chain in order to operate. It branches out and then runs parallel to the old blockchain. Since this is not a completely new system, you get to carry over your coins to the new chain. All the basic rules of the system will stay the same and the system will still propagate blocks and transactions. There was an identical blockchain until the split. Moving forward there would be a change in the two blockchains and they will not be merged. There is no way to go back with this kind of change and the new blockchain does not communicate with the parallel old blockchain. Hard forks are, in essence, backward-incompatible software updates.
Now with soft forks, they are backward-compatible software updates. This means that they can still communicate with the nodes that haven’t been updated. It is not such a drastic change that there can’t be any communication between the two and a big split is not necessary. If there are those that do not agree with the change, it can still happen and everyone can stay on the same blockchain. A soft fork happens when the addition of the new rule doesn’t clash with any of the previous rules.
Technically there is no better option since they both serve a different purpose. However, a hard fork can cause a divide in a community, which is not something that most platforms want. If there is an option to limit the amount of hard forks a system goes through, this will be the most ideal option. A soft fork is far gentler and keeps the community intact even when there is a change that takes place. It is for this reason that Polkadot is set up in a way that will allow changes to happen, but still prevent any hard forks.
The DOT Token
The DOT token has already been mentioned a few times when talking about Polkadot. This is just the native token that can be used with the platform. Just like how Bitcoin has the bitcoin and Ethereum has the ether. Most blockchain platforms must have a token in order for it to function properly and work with the platform. It is an integral part of the platform and to the systems that hold it together.
The DOT is not just used as a means for trading with the platform. You will need to do anything on the platform so there are multiple functions for it. When a user has a DOT they have governance rights over the platform. The DOT acts like how buying a share in a company would be. The more shares you have the more of a say you have. It also shows who is very serious about being involved in Polkadot. If someone really believes in it and wants to be a part of what is going on they would be more motivated to buy in and have a stake in it. If you have a stake in Polkadot you will have a say in a variety of different decisions. This will include deciding on the fees, deployment or the removal of certain chains, and the network upgrades. Those that have DOTs will be able to vote on all of these scenarios. As the platform progresses, there will be more need for this and more decisions to be made. This system allows decisions to be made that will best benefit the majority of users.
DOT can be used as the staking process of the PoS system. DOT cannot be mined. There has been a lot of talk about mining in blockchain platforms, but that is because those platforms are working on a PoW system. Ethereum is moving over to a PoS system, which means that there will be no mining to get new coins. Polkadot is already there. Since it runs on the PoS system there is no mining in order to get coins. You have to buy it with real-world currency and that is how you enter the system. The amount to buy will determine the stake you have in the Polkadot platform. If someone wants to validate transactions or perform any of the four roles that have been mentioned at the beginning of the chapter, they would have to stake their DOTs. These would then be frozen until the action is completed successfully. If the user tries to go against the rules of the system, their stake would be lost and they would not be able to recover it.
The other thing that DOT will be needed for in the Polkadot platform is for something called bonding. When new parachains are introduced to the system the tokens are committed to the bonding of the chain to the system. Here is where they stay for a specific time period. If the project comes to an end, it is no longer maintained, or there is a chain that is no longer needed, the binding ends. The DOT tokens are released and returned to the person that owns them. The chain will then no longer exist because the project is no longer there. This also means that the more DOT tokens you have, the more projects you can fund and be a part of (Binance Academy, 2021b).
The Applications Being Built on Polkadot
With the way Polkadot has been developed, there are plenty of exciting projects that are being built into the system. This is one of the draws to the platform. People can go on and build their own projects into the system and because of this there have been lots of new additions in a short space of time. People are flocking towards the Polkadot platform because this is something that is not available anywhere else. We will be going through some of the exciting projects that have already been built or are in the pipeline. Unfortunately, we won’t be able to go through all of them because that would be a book on its own. Take a look at some of the exciting applications and projects there are on the Polkadot platform.
Kusama
This is an app that has been built by the same developers who are behind the Polkadot platform. This project utilizes the same codebase as Polkadot, so it is a very close cousin to the original platform. The reason that this was created is to give users a place to test out their projects. It creates an ideal environment to test fast paced and innovative projects and be able to pre-check before launching them in the main platform. Developers can test and try out their ideas here as well as fine-tune the development of the project (Block Telegraph, 2021).
Once the creator of the project is happy with it, they can either choose to move it to the main Polkadot blockchain, or they can stay on Kusama. The choice is theirs because Kusama is a fully functional parachain that even comes with its own token. This means that it is governed by holders of the KSM token. If the creator of the project wants to stay and be governed by Kusama rather than the Polkadot main chain then they can stay and become part of that community.
If you wanted to enter the system, you would need to enter your project into the system by creating a Kusama account. This is a pretty easy and straightforward task as it doesn't need admin permission and pretty much anyone will be able to do it and have access to the platform. Once you do that, you will need to pay in a certain amount of KSM tokens, and then you can get started. Setting up your project on Kusama is much cheaper than if you were to set it up on Polkadot. This is why it is a better alternative for those that are first time users of the platform and just want to work on developing their project. This will also mean that the fee for transactions and other actions on the platform will also be much cheaper. You can get KSM tokens by buying DOT tokens and claim the proportional amount of KSM tokens or you could just go onto an exchange site and buy them directly from there with dollars.
This is a great platform to get connected to for young startups as it will not take a lot of resources to get started. Even if the user just wants to test out an idea, this creates the opportunity for that. This is something that is much needed in the blockchain world. There will most likely be many new and exciting innovations that come out of this platform.
Moonbeam
This is a platform that is compatible with Ethereum and works with it for the deployment of smart contracts. It will also provide the Polkadot users with the toolkit available in the Ethereum platform by creating a bridge between the two platforms. Moonbeam also has its own token that the users can use for smart contract execution and the payment of fees, this token is called a Glimmer.
This has not been launched as yet, but it should be out by the second half of 2021. From then people can access the platform and all the benefits. This is going to be one of the first bridges from Polkadot and will pave the way for a lot more connections in the future.
Acala
This is considered as one of the most successful projects to be launched on the Polkadot platform. The goal of this is to bring in a Decentralized Financial Consortium into the Polkadot platform. Decentralized finance is not something to be looked over. With the big move towards it, it would only make sense to add something to the platform that would be able to facilitate it.
Acala has its own token and is run the same as most other decentralized finance platforms. It is owned and run by those that have the token. The token holders have the sole power to validate, vote on changes, and run the network. This system is a dual protocol system, consisting of the Honzol Protocol and the Homa Protocol. These two work together to allow the system to run as it should. The Honzol Protocol is responsible for use and creation of a stable coin that will be used within the Polkadot platform. It will enable the participants to gain rewards in dollars, instead of just a cryptocurrency. This is one of the first of its kind and might end up changing the game in the crypto world. The Homa Protocol allows the user to use their DOT tokens while they are being staked. Currently the user will have to stake their coins and it would be frozen until the action that froze the coins has been completed. Only once the token has been released is the user allowed to take the coin out and use it. This protocol allows liquidity for the DOT tokens. This is a completely different innovation that could change the way PoS systems work in the future (Blaize, 2020).
Edgeware
This is a substrate-based chain that will allow the creation of smart contracts to happen within the ecosystem. The users will be able to perform voting and funding processes within the platform. The goal of this platform is to have a test environment for the governance of the chains. It is set up in such a way that it allows the system to implement changes and improvements on its own and then lets the users vote on them. This will allow the program to continually better itself without it having to stop or have any drastic changes being made.
Edgware offers blockchain modularity, which means that the different parts of the blockchain are separated into modules. When a change or modification needs to happen it only affects one module and the other remains unaffected and can continue on as normal. This also allows the system to make quick changes and improvements. The overall platform will improve at a much faster rate because of this system.
Polkastarter
Polkastarter is a fundraising protocol on the platform. This will allow everyone a fair opportunity to invest into new projects as they arise. This will also help with the development of new projects. Whenever there is a new development or project idea, there needs to be some funding. Often there are people who want to build something, but do not have the funds to do so. This a solution to that problem and it will also allow investors the opportunity to easily know what the new investment opportunities are.
In order to be part of this platform, you would need to be in possession of the Polkastarter token. This will allow you access to potential investments before everyone else. When a new project hits the market, it can explode and this makes it harder for investors to put in smaller amounts of money. Doing it this way allows financial community involvement and it is still a fair system to work with.
If someone is looking to invest, but does not know how to get into it, this platform will benefit them. It will also benefit the developers as they know that they have a chance to get investors. Often finding investors can be a difficult process because you have to seek out those that are willing to part with their money for the opportunity to invest. This platform allows the right people to meet each other to allow new ideas and innovations to grow and form.
Bridge Mutual
There is no doubt that with the rise in decentralized finance options that there will be a rise in hackers that are trying to access the platforms and steal currency. With this knowledge and risk there needs to be some way for people to lower the risk of them losing everything in the event of this happening. Bridge Mutual is a sort of insurance type platform where people have access to a decentralized risk coverage platform.
Since it is decentralized, there is no one entity that is handing out loans or insurance covers. It is still a peer-to-peer system, but the incentive to give out loans will be the interest earned. People will be able to put their tokens into pools and then collect interest while it is just sitting there. If someone claims from the insurance, the platform will use assets from the other platforms in the system to generate extra yield for those who have put money into the insurance pool. This is a system that will allow people to feel safer and prevent losing everything should something bad happen. Although the systems are incredibly safe, there is always a risk. The better technology gets, the better people get at hacking. It is just the way things work so it is better to be prepared than to be blindsided later on down the road.
Polkaswap
On the Ethereum platform there is a protocol called Uniswap. This is a decentralized exchange system that allows people to trade their tokens of various networks for other tokens from a different network. This creates liquidity for those that want to be involved in the cryptocurrency market. Usually, users would have to take their tokens and use a centralized exchange platform if they wanted to trade with a different platform. Uniswap is a much easier method of exchanging tokens. The problem is that, since Ethereum has gotten clogged with all the transactions taking place, the fees involved with making these exchanges have really increased. It has gotten to the point where it is not worth it to exchange your tokens because you would be losing a lot of currency in the process. Polkaswap is a competing service that offers a solution to this.
There is almost no difference between Polkaswap and Uniswap as they perform the same function. However, the transaction will happen a lot faster and the fees will be much lower on Polkaswap. This is because of the type of blockchain it runs on. The other difference is that because it is built on a cross chain decentralized exchange system, Polkaswap can swap with any platform. It achieves liquidity for various products over multiple platforms. Uniswap is quite limited in this area and there needed to be a better solution put in place. In the future, Polkaswap could easily be the number one platform for decentralized finance exchanges and transactions.
Interoperability Between Different Chains
